## PDF page 6
Pr  e  f  a  c  e






This is the 16th yearly report on Research Publications and Research Profiles for the Faculty
of Medicine & Health Sciences, United Arab Emirates University.  This volume is a continua-
tion of a series of annual reports, dating back to our very first report in 1991.


As usual, every year, we also try to upgrade and maintain the quality of this directory and
this year is no exception. We have continue              d to include the Departmental Profiles and the
Reports from the Research Priority Groups. We also included short reports on two major
conferences this year; the International Conference on Advances in Diabetes Mellitus & its
Complications, organized by the Diabetes Research Group, and the Cancer Research Day,
organized by the Oncology Research Group. A special report on Student Medical Research
is also included.


As always, it is a pleasure to thank those individuals, who have put this report together, to
cater to the individual layouts and to consolidate all the sections into one book. Mrs Ivanna
Lizarriturri and Ms Shaikha al Butti ably performed that work. Mr Rajagopalan, as always,
took care of editing the initial departmental contribution and the communication between
all parties. Mr Ashook took numerous pictures and Mr Mohamed Zaggar took care of the
translation.




Professor Wim Lammers                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      Professor M Lukic
              Editor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        Associate Dean for Research

## PDF page 8
Dean's Message


Dear Colleagues,

A sincer e thank you to faculty, students and tech nical staf f           for a ver  y high level of           r esearch
pr oductivity during the calendar year 2006.   Thanks again to W im Lammers for editing
the diverse infor mation about our grants and publications into  a cohesive document that en-
ables FMHS r esearch ef forts to be communicated  ef fectively both inter  nally and exter  nally.

Grant sponsorship at FMHS involves a wide ra nge of          benefactors, without whom the re-
search could not be conducted.   Despite budgetar  y constraints, inter nal funding at FMHS
and UAE University happily remains a priority, vital to the attraction and retention of
high quality investigators.  Contributions fr om bodies such as Sheikh Hamdan Awards for
Medical Science, Ter r y Fox Cancer Funds in Dubai and Abu Dhabi and the Michael J
Fox Foundation ar e gr eatly appr eciated.  The Br itish Council in the UAE continues to en-
able student r esearch electives to the Un ited Kingdom in partnership with FMHS  .

The Diabetes and Cardiovascular Gr oup held a ver  y successful confer ence in the Spring of
2006.  An important sequel to the 2005 Inter national Neur osciences meeting was our ac-
ceptance as Inter national Brain Research Or  ganisation (IBRO) members and our convening
the first IBRO School in the Middle East her e in Al Ain.

The foundation cohort of          Master of          Medical Science graduate students has been accepted.
They should begin their r esearch activities shortly in both Medical Micr obiolog y & Immu-
nolog y and Pharmacolog y & Toxicolog y.  We understand that a future PhD program in
Micr obiolog  y has str ong UAEU support.

Student research remains a distinguishing feature of          this Medical College.  Our dedicated
students and their r esearch mentors, both faculty and staf f, should be suitably pr oud of            their
continued high level of             achievement during 2006.

The UAE University Research Affairs sector led by Dr. Maitha Al Shamsi and the
FMHS research administration team led by Professor Mio Lukic are war mly appreciated
by all FMHS r esearchers!

It is the challenge of         all good biomedical r esearch to enhance the quality of         patient care
thr ough impr oved diagnosis or better tr eatment.   We must continually strive to enhance our
clinical interface and to strengthen partnerships within and outside UAE if        we are to
achieve this objective.

                                                                                  S Geor  ge Car  ruthers, MD
                                                                                                Dean

embryos, newer efforts are directed at how ex-

Protein Misfolding and Neurodegenerative                           tests for PD and related diseases, and for testing thera-
Diseases  (Dr. O. M. A. El-Agnaf)                                  peutic agents aimed at preventing or reversing the ag-
Pathological studies in human neurodegenerative                    gregation of alpha-synuclein.
diseases such as Alzheimer's disease (AD), Parkin-
son’s disease (PD), dementia with Lewy bodies
(DLB), the prion dementias (e.g. mad cow disease
and its equivalent, CJD, in humans), British de-
mentia and Huntington’s disease, have revealed
abundant protein deposits (‘amyloid’) in the af-
fected neurones. There is now substantial evi-
dence from molecular genetics, transgenic animal
and the biochemical studies to suggest that the
conversion of these amyloid proteins from soluble
monomers to aggregated, insoluble forms in the
brain is a key event in the pathogenesis of these
diseases.  This led us to hypothesize that small
molecules that can block and/or reverse amyloid
protein aggregation, particularly in its early stages,
would provide an attractive therapeutic approach
for targeting the underlying disease progression of
neurodegenerative disease.

Our research is concerned with the mechanism of
formation and deposition of protein aggregates in
these various diseases, and on the potential rela-
tionship between protein aggregation, neurode-
generation and cell loss. We are also very inter-
ested in novel approaches to improved diagnosis
and therapy of these diseases.  A further aspect of
our research is the development of novel tech-
niques for detecting amyloid aggregates, particu-
larly in their early stages. We are assessing these
methods for the detection of aggregated forms of
Abeta and alpha-synuclein in CSF and plasma sam-
ples from AD, PD and DLB patients as an aid to                     “Frog of the Year” Rana draytonii and a Schiffer-Edmundsen

volved in the development of peptide and non-                      nature of its α-helical conformation.
peptide inhibitors of amyloid aggregation and tox-
icity as potential novel therapeutics for these neu-
rodegenerative diseases.

RESEARCH PROFILE                                                  that is independent of vascular complications. We
Members of Staff of the Physiology Department
have interests in neuroscience and muscle                         are investigating the cellular and sub-cellular
(including skeletal, cardiac and smooth muscle).                  mechanisms which may be responsible for con-
The experimental work includes normal physiol-                    tractility and heart rhythm defects in the diabetic
ogy as well as patho-physiology, particularly in re-              heart. We employ a variety of techniques including
lation to the peripheral nerves, autonomic nervous                biotelemetry, electrophysiology, molecular biology
system, and heart in the diabetic state, which is a               and confocal microscopy.
common disorder in the United Arab Emirates.
The work requires complex electrophysiological
and cellular methodologies, as well as electron
microscopy, using in vitro and in vivo techniques.


AGING AND NEURODEGENERATIVE
DISORDERS
Studies on aging and neurodegenerative disorders
have focused on synaptic plasticity and synaptic
remodelling, particularly at the nerve-muscle junc-
tion in various peripheral neuropathies including
those caused by diabetes, aging and heavy metal
poisoning. Electrophysiological techniques and elec-
tronmicroscopy are used in combination to focus
on changes in muscular performance in these condi-
tions.  It is hypothesised that free radicals are in-
volved in these nerve injuries and antioxidants like
vitamin E & C may have a protective role.   Studies               Use of immunoconfocal microscopy to label beta-tubulin
on the cerebral microcirculation include changes in               (green) and microtubule-associated protein 4 (red) in ven-
the susceptibilty to thrombus formation in the                    tricular myocytes.
brain, and the effects of metals, aspirin and heat.

MUSCLE
The dynamics of the control of the muscles is an-
other focus of activity involving electrophysiology
and mathematical modelling, and seeks explain the
influence of various physiological and pathological
conditions on changes in muscle performance. In
particular, the effects of chronic ischemia and mus-
cle fatigue are currently being investigated. The
model of chronic ischemia induced in vivo by
femoral artery occlusion is employed; the effects
of exogenously applied Neuropeptide-Y (NPY) are
of interest because the angiogenesis and revascu-
larisation it can induce are used as tools to alter                                       Research Lab
the dynamic properties of skeletal muscle.
Changes in muscle dynamics are estimated by
monitoring various parameters i.e. EMG, twitch
force, force/time integral, etc. during various non-
fatiguing and fatiguing protocols induced by electri-
cal stimulation of muscle nerves and ventral spinal
roots. The results are relevant for better under-
standing of changes of intra-muscle dynamics in
various conditions and probing the possibility that
NPY may be a potential drug in treatment of
ischemic disease.

The Cellular Mechanism of Robinow Syndrome .
Robinow Syndrome is a recessive disease found in many parts of the Middle East including Oman and Saudi Arabia and is caused by
mutations in ROR2 gene.  Wild type ROR2 protein traffic normally to the plasma membrane of the cell (panel A) whereas Robinow
Syndrome causing mutations result in the retention of the mutant proteins in the endoplasmic reticulum (panel B).  This picture  from
Dr. B. Ali’s work on the cellular mechanisms of genetic diseases.

## PDF page 110
The International Conference on Advances in
Diabetes Mellitus & its Complications
March 6-9, 2006






The International Conference on Advances in                   There were also trips to Abu Dhabi and Dubai.
Diabetes Mellitus & its Complications was held                The last day of the conference was capped by a
under the patronage of His Excellency Sheikh                  dinner cruise in Dubai Creek prior to the de-
Nahayan Mubarak Al Nahayan, Minister of Edu-                  parture of delegates.
cation and Chancellor, United Arab Emirates
University on March 6 - 9, 2006 at the Hilton                 The organization of the conference was able to
Hotel in Al Ain. This International Conference                succeed because of the relentless effort and sup-
was organized by the Diabetes and Cardiovas-                  port of the all members of the Organizing Com-
cular Research Group, Faculty of Medicine &                   mittee, faculty, staff and the administration of
Health Sciences, UAE University.                              the Faculty of Medicine & Health Sciences and
                                                              the University. The support of all of our spon-
The conference attracted more than 650 dele-                  sors is much appreciated.
gates, including distinguished diabetes experts
from several countries including Algeria, Australia,
Bahrain, Belgium, Canada, Cameroon, China,
Czech Republic, Egypt, Finland, Gambia, Ghana,                Prof Ernest Adeghate
Germany, Greece, Hungary, India, Iran, Iraq, Ja-              on behalf of the committees
pan, Jordan, Kuwait, Morocco, Oman, Pakistan,
Palestine, Qatar, Russia, Saudi Arabia, Spain, Su-
dan, Sweden, Tunisia, Ukraine, United Arab Emir-
ates, United Kingdom and the United States of
America.

This international conference was opened by
His Excellency Sheikh Nahayan Mubarak Al Na-
hayan. Distinguished invited speakers gave key-
note presentations on all areas of diabetes in-
cluding but not limited to         the epidemiology of
diabetes, metabolic syndrome, nutrition and
diabetes, immunology of diabetes and pancreas
transplantation, diabetic retinopathy, nephropa-
thy, and neuropathy. In addition to keynote
presentations, workshop    s on current manage-
ment issues were also organized.

The abstracts of the presentations were pub-
lished in the International Journal of Diabetes
and Metabolism while full length articles of the
papers presented were published in the Annals
of New York Academy of Sciences (Volume
1084, November 2006).

The conference had a rich social program which
includes a tour of the garden city of Al Ain and its
museums, a gala dinner at the Mercure Hotel in
Jebel Hafeet and a cultural night at the Hilton Hotel.
